\displaystyle\text{by the above bound on }\lVert X\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4n}{d}

\displaystyle\text{since }\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})^{2}\leqslant 1.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{H}_{W}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\mathbf{G}_{W}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\mathbf{G}_{W}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leqslant\frac{4n}{d}.

2.

Recall from Section
D.2.3
the Taylor series expansion of
\kappa
:

\kappa(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum^{\infty}_{r=0}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)_{r}}{r!+2rr!}(\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime})^{2r+2}.

Hence,

\mathbf{H}=\frac{1}{4}XX^{\top}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum^{\infty}_{r=0}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)_{r}}{r!+2rr!}\left(XX^{\top}\right)^{\odot(2r+2)}=\frac{1}{4}XX^{\top}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\left(XX^{\top}\right)^{\odot 2}+...\right),

where the superscript
\odot(2r+2)
denotes the
(2r+2)
-times Hadamard product. Here,
XX^{\top}
is clearly positive semi-definite, and by Schur product theorem
(
Horn and Johnson 2013
, p.479, Theorem 7.5.3)
, we know that Hadamard products of positive semi-definite matrices are positive semi-definite, so each summand is positive semi-definite, and so just considering the first term
\frac{1}{4}XX^{\top}
and denoting the minimum eigenvalue of
XX^{\top}
by
\mu_{\min}
, we have
\bm{\lambda}_{\min}\geqslant\frac{1}{4}\mu_{\min}
. But by
(
Vershynin 2018
, p.91, Theorem 4.6.1)
, the singular value of
\sqrt{d}X
is lower bounded by
\sqrt{n}-\frac{C}{2}(\sqrt{d}+t)
with probability at least
1-2e^{-t^{2}}
for any
t\geqslant 0
, where
C>0
is an absolute constant. Letting
t=\sqrt{d}
, the singular value of
\sqrt{d}X
is lower bounded by
\sqrt{n}-C\sqrt{d}\geqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{5}}\sqrt{n}
(using Assumption
2
2
) with probability at least
1-2e^{-d}
. This means that, with probability at least
1-2e^{-d}
,
\mu_{\min}\geqslant\frac{4n}{5d}
. Hence
\bm{\lambda}_{\min}\geqslant\frac{n}{5d}
. We note that, again,
2e^{-d}\leqslant\frac{\delta}{6}
by Assumption
2
1
.

The events of parts
1
and
2
each have probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{6}
, so by the union bound, the event on which both parts are satisfied has probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3}
. Now we look for the event
E_{2}\subseteq E_{1}
on which the events of this Lemma hold, and by union bound, we have
\mathbb{P}(E_{2})\geqslant 1-\frac{2\delta}{3}
.
∎

D.3.3
Randomness due to both Weight Initialization and Sampling

Finally, we present some results that hold with high probability, in which the randomness comes both from the weights and the samples.

Lemma 18
.

We have the following high-probability events:

1.

If Assumptions
2
1
&
2
are satisfied, the minimum eigenvalue of the initial NTK matrix is bounded from below with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{6}
:

\bm{\lambda}_{0,\min}\geqslant\frac{n}{10d}.

2.

Define, for each
u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}
,

V_{u}=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}.

If all the conditions in Assumption
2
is satisfied, then with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{6}
, for all
u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}
,

\left\lVert V_{u}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}<8\sqrt{\frac{\log(nu)}{\lfloor\frac{n}{u}\rfloor}}.

3.

If all the conditions in Assumption
2
is satisfied, then we have

Hence, if all the conditions in Assumption
2
are satisfied, then there is an event
E_{3}\subseteq E_{2}
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{3})\geqslant 1-\delta
on which parts
1
and
2
occur simultaneously.

Proof.

1.

Recall from Section
D.2.2
that we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{w}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left[\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{w}}\right]=\frac{1}{m}\mathbf{H}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{H}_{0}=\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)}.

For each
j=1,...,m
, apply (
B.1
), and note that
\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})^{2}\leqslant 1
and apply Lemma
17
1
for
\lVert X\rVert_{2}
to see that

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\left\lVert(XX^{\top})\odot(\phi^{\prime}(X\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)^{\top})\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)X^{\top}))\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\lVert X\rVert_{2}^{2}}{m}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4n}{md}.

Hence, recalling from Lemma
17
2
that we have
\bm{\lambda}_{\min}\geqslant\frac{n}{5d}
and using the Matrix Chernoff inequality (
B.2
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\lambda}_{0,\min}\leqslant\frac{n}{10d}\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\lambda}_{0,\min}\leqslant\frac{\bm{\lambda}_{\min}}{2}\right)\leqslant n\left(\sqrt{2}e\right)^{-\frac{md\bm{\lambda}_{\min}}{8n}}\leqslant n\left(\sqrt{2}e\right)^{-\frac{m}{40}}.

We note that
n\left(\sqrt{2}e\right)^{-\frac{m}{40}}\leqslant\frac{\delta}{6}
by Assumption
2
2
.

2.

For each
u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\sum^{n}_{i_{1},...,i_{u}=1}G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}})[\mathbf{H}_{0}]_{i_{1},i_{2}}...[\mathbf{H}_{0}]_{i_{u-1},i_{u}}y_{i_{u}}.

Here,
[\mathbf{H}_{0}]_{i,i^{\prime}}=\langle G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\rangle_{\text{F}}=\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})
, so

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\sum^{n}_{i_{1},...,i_{u}=1}G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}})\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}},\mathbf{x}_{i_{2}})...\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{u-1}},\mathbf{x}_{i_{u}})y_{i_{u}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\sum^{n}_{i_{1},...,i_{u}=1}G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}})y_{i_{u}}\prod_{c=1}^{u-1}\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{c}},\mathbf{x}_{i_{c+1}})

Defining
\Upsilon_{u}:(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})^{u}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m\times d}
as

\Upsilon_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{1},y_{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{u},y_{u}))=G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{1})\prod^{u-1}_{c=1}\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{c},\mathbf{x}_{c+1})y_{u}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2},

we clearly have
\mathbb{E}[\Upsilon_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{1},y_{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{u},y_{u}))]=0
and that

\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0}\,H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\sum_{i_{1},...,i_{u}=1}\Upsilon_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}},y_{i_{1}}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{i_{u}},y_{i_{u}})),

i.e., we have a V-statistic (c.f. Section
B.6
). We actually construct a symmetric version
\bar{\Upsilon}_{u}:(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})^{u}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m\times d}
of
\Upsilon_{u}
by

\bar{\Upsilon}_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{1},y_{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{u},y_{u}))=\frac{1}{u!}\sum_{*}\Upsilon_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}},y_{i_{1}}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{i_{u}},y_{i_{u}})),

where the sum
\sum_{*}
is over the
u!
permutations
\{i_{1},...,i_{u}\}
of
\{1,...,u\}
. Then it is easy to see that we still have
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Upsilon}_{u}]=0
and

V_{u}=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}=\frac{1}{n^{u}}\sum^{n}_{i_{1},...,i_{u}=1}\bar{\Upsilon}_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}},y_{i_{1}}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{i_{u}},y_{i_{u}})).

Note that we have, almost surely for all
u
-tuples
((\mathbf{x}_{1},y_{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{u},y_{u}))
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\Upsilon}_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{1},y_{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{u},y_{u}))\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{u!}\sum_{*}\lVert\Upsilon_{u}((\mathbf{x}_{i_{1}},y_{i_{1}}),...,(\mathbf{x}_{i_{u}},y_{i_{u}}))\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert G_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{0})\rVert_{\text{F}}\prod^{u-1}_{c=1}\lvert\kappa_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{c},\mathbf{x}_{c+1})\rvert\lvert y_{u}\rvert+\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\sqrt{\langle H_{0}^{u}\zeta_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\underbrace{\lVert H_{0}\rVert_{2}^{u-\frac{1}{2}}}_{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:non_random}\ref{H_W}}}\underbrace{\lVert f^{*}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{\ref{ass:f^*bound}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\frac{1}{(2d)^{u-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2.

Hence, from Proposition
14
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\lVert V_{u}\rVert_{\text{F}}\geqslant 8\sqrt{\frac{\log(nu)}{\lfloor\frac{n}{u}\rfloor}}\right)\leqslant\frac{2}{n}.

Taking a union bound over
u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\lVert V_{u}\rVert_{\text{F}}\geqslant 8\sqrt{\frac{\log(nu)}{\lfloor\frac{n}{u}\rfloor}}\text{ for some }u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}\right)\leqslant\frac{2U_{\varepsilon}}{n}.

We note that
\frac{2U_{\varepsilon}}{n}\leqslant\frac{\delta}{6}
by Assumption
2
3
.

3.

The events of parts
1
and
2
each have probabilities at least
1-\frac{\delta}{6}
, so by union bound,
E_{3}\subseteq E_{2}
on which the events of this Lemma all hold satisfies
\mathbb{P}(E_{3})\geqslant 1-\delta
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Overfitting

In this section, we assume that we are on the high-probability event
E_{3}
from Lemma
18
in Appendix
D.3
, and we show that the empirical risk
\lVert\mathbf{y}-\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{t}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
is small. Our strategy will be to use real induction (c.f. Appendix
B.5
) on
t
to get a bound on
\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
. To that end, we give the following definition.

Definition 19
.

Define a subset
\hat{S}
of
[0,\infty)
as the collection of
t\in[0,\infty)
such that, for each
j=1,...,m
,

\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)-\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}<32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}.

Our goal is to show a bound on
\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. We first prove a few results that hold for
t\in\hat{S}
.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2
1
&
2
and
3
1
are satisfied, and suppose that
t\in\hat{S}
.

1.

The spectral norm of the NTK matrix does not move much:

\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{4n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{\sqrt{md}}.

2.

The minimum eigenvalue of
\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}
is bounded from below:

\hat{\bm{\lambda}}_{t,\min}>\frac{n}{16d},

which implies

\lVert\nabla_{W}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\geqslant\frac{1}{4n^{2}}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

3.

The gradient of the norm of the error vector is bounded from above by a negative number:

\frac{d\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\leqslant-\frac{1}{8d}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}.

4.

The norm of the error vector decays exponentially:

\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{8d}\right).

Proof.

1.

See that, using (
B.1
), (
B.1
) and Lemma
17
1
,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}*X^{\top})^{\top}(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}*X^{\top})-(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}*X^{\top})^{\top}(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}*X^{\top})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert(XX^{\top})\odot(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\lVert X\rVert_{2}^{2}}{m}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{W}(t)^{\top})\phi^{\prime}(\hat{W}(t)\mathbf{x}_{i})-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}W(0)^{\top})\phi^{\prime}(W(0)\mathbf{x}_{i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4n}{dm}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})^{2}-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{4n}{dm}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\left\{\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\right\}.

Here, for each
i=1,...,n
and
j=1,...,m
, in order for
\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})
, there must be some
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
on the weight trajectory, such that
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i}=0
and

\lVert\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}\leqslant 32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}.

But by Lemma
16
3
, there only exist at most
\sqrt{md}(34+\sqrt{\log m})
neurons such that this happens. Hence,

\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{4n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{\sqrt{md}}.

2.

See that

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\lambda}}_{t,\min}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\inf_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}-\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\lVert(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0})\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\hat{\bm{\lambda}}_{0,\min}-\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{n}{10d}-\frac{4n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{\sqrt{md}}

by Lemma
18
1
& part
1

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{n}{16d}

by Assumption
3
1

as required. Then using this, see that

\lVert\nabla_{W}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}^{2}=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}=\frac{4}{n^{2}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\geqslant\frac{1}{4nd}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

3.

Differentiate both sides of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}=\frac{1}{n}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}
with respect to
t
and apply the chain rule to obtain

\frac{d\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}}{dt}=\frac{2}{n}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\frac{d\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\qquad\implies\qquad\frac{d\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}=\frac{n}{2\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}\frac{d\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}}{dt}.

We apply the chain rule and part
2
to see that

\frac{d\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}}{dt}=\left\langle\nabla_{W}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t},\frac{d\hat{W}}{dt}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}=-\lVert\nabla_{W}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}^{2}\leqslant-\frac{1}{4nd}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}

Hence, substituting into above,

\frac{d\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\leqslant-\frac{1}{8d}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}.

4.

We apply Grönwall’s inequality and the fact that
\lVert\bm{\xi}_{0}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\mathbf{y}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\sqrt{n}
to see that

\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\bm{\xi}_{0}\rVert_{2}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{8d}\right)\leqslant\sqrt{n}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{8d}\right).

∎

Finally, we prove that
\hat{S}\in[0,\infty)
is inductive. Then we know from Appendix
B.5
that
\hat{S}=[0,\infty)
.

Theorem 21
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2
1
&
2
and
3
1
are satisfied. Then
\hat{S}
is inductive.

Proof.

We prove each of (RI1), (RI2) and (RI3) in Appendix
B.5
for the set
\hat{S}
.

1.

Obvious.

2.

Fix some
T\geqslant 0
, and suppose that
T\in\hat{S}
. Then we want to show that there exists some
\gamma>0
such that
[T,T+\gamma]\subseteq\hat{S}
.
Since
T\in\hat{S}
, we have
\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}<32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}
for each
j=1,...,m
. Define

\gamma_{j}=4d-\frac{\sqrt{md}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}}{8}.

Then
\gamma_{j}>0
, and for all
t\in[T,T+\gamma_{j}]
,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)-\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\int^{t}_{T}\frac{d\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}}{dt}dt\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\int^{t}_{T}\lVert\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j}}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}\rVert_{2}dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\frac{2}{n}\int^{t}_{T}\lVert\mathbf{G}_{\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\frac{4}{\sqrt{mnd}}\int^{t}_{T}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}dt

by Lemma
17
1

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+\frac{4(t-T)}{\sqrt{md}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}+16\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}

\displaystyle<32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}.

Now take
\gamma=\min_{j\in\{1,...,m\}}\gamma_{j}
. Then
[T,T+\gamma]\subseteq\hat{S}
as required.

3.

Fix some
T\geqslant 0
and suppose that
[0,T)\subseteq\hat{S}
. Then we want to show that
T\in\hat{S}
. See that, for each
j\in\{1,...,m\}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{d\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}}{dt}dt\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}-\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j}}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{t}dt\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\mathbf{G}_{\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}dt\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{\sqrt{mnd}}\int^{T}_{0}\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}dt

Lemma
17
1

\displaystyle<\frac{4}{\sqrt{md}}\int^{T}_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{t}{8d}\right)dt\quad

Lemma
20
4

\displaystyle\leqslant 32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}.

So
T\in\hat{S}
.

Since
\hat{S}
satisfies all of (RI1), (RI2) and (RI3),
\hat{S}
is inductive.
∎

See
7

Proof.

Theorem
21
implies that we can run gradient flow as long as we want and ensure that the empirical risk follows Lemma
20
4
.

So only the last statement requires attention. We know from Lemma
15
1
that the maximum value of
\lambda_{\varepsilon}
is
\frac{1}{4d}
, which means that the minimum value of
T_{\varepsilon}
is
8d\log\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)
. Hence,

\mathbf{R}(\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}})\leqslant\exp\left(-2\log\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)=\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\leqslant\varepsilon

as required.
∎

D.5
Proof of Small Approximation Error

In this section, we assume that we are still on the high-probability event
E_{3}
from Lemma
18
in Appendix
D.3
, and we show that the approximation error
\lVert f^{\star}-f_{t}\rVert_{2}=\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}
is small, i.e., less than our desired level
\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}
, with the other
\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}
to come from the estimation error in Appendix
D.6
.

Our strategy will be to use real induction (c.f. Appendix
B.5
) on
t
to get a bound on
\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}
for some
m
that depends on
\varepsilon
. First, recalling the definition of
L_{\varepsilon}
from (
4.1
), note that there exists some time
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
(which may be
\infty
) defined as

T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}=\min\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant 2\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\},

(D.1)

i.e., the first time that
\lVert\zeta^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
accounts for less than half of
\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}
. It may be that
\lVert\zeta_{t}^{L_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}
will never account for less than half of
\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}
, in which case we will have
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}=\infty
. The purpose of
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
is to ensure that we have approximation error bounded by
\varepsilon
before we hit
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
, so it is no problem for
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
to be infinite.

Definition 22
.

Define a subset
S_{\varepsilon}
of
[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
as the collection of
t\in[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
such that, for each
j=1,...,m
,

\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}<\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}.

We first prove a few results that hold for
t\in S_{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 23
.

Suppose that Assumption
2
1
and Assumption
3
2
are satisfied, and that
t\in S_{\varepsilon}
.

1.

We have

\lVert H_{t}-H_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}).

2.

We have

\lVert\nabla_{W}R_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\geqslant\lambda_{\varepsilon}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

3.

We have

\frac{d\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\leqslant-\frac{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}{2}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}.

4.

We have

\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\varepsilon}t\right).

Proof.

1.

First see that

\displaystyle(H_{t}-H_{0})f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\left[\left(\langle G_{t}(\mathbf{x}),G_{t}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\rangle_{\text{F}}-\langle G_{0}(\mathbf{x}),G_{0}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\rangle_{\text{F}}\right)f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left(\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right)f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right].

We use the same linear operator
\Xi
as in the proof of Lemma
15
2
, which we recall to be

\Xi(f)(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime}f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})],

and we also recall that
\lVert\Xi\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2d}
. Now applying Lemma
12
, we see that

\displaystyle\lVert H_{t}-H_{0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2d}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\left\lvert\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left(\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})^{2}-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})^{2}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2d}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left\lvert\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})^{2}-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})^{2}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2d}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\left\{\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})\right\}.

Here, for each
j=1,...,m
, in order for
\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})
, there must be some
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
on the weight trajectory, such that
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}=0
and

\lVert\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}.

But by Lemma
16
4
, there only exist at most
\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{d}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m})
neurons such that this happens. Hence,

\lVert H_{t}-H_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}).

2.

See that

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{W}R_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert 2\langle G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert^{2}_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=4\langle\zeta_{t},H_{t}\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}

\displaystyle=4\langle\zeta_{t},H\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}+4\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{0}-H)\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}+4\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\underbrace{4\langle\zeta_{t},H\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}}_{\text{(a)}}-\underbrace{4\lvert\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{0}-H)\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rvert}_{\text{(b)}}-\underbrace{4\lvert\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rvert}_{\text{(c)}}.

We look at (a), (b) and (c) separately.

(a)

Recall that
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
is defined as

T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}=\min\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:\lVert\zeta_{t}^{L_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\tilde{\zeta}_{t}^{L_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\}=\min\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}:\lVert\zeta_{t}^{L_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\}.

Since
t\leqslant T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
, we have

4\langle\zeta_{t},H\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}=4\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\lambda_{l}\langle\zeta_{t},\varphi_{l}\rangle_{2}^{2}\geqslant 4\sum^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{l=1}\lambda_{l}\langle\zeta_{t},\varphi_{l}\rangle_{2}^{2}\geqslant 4\lambda_{\varepsilon}\lVert\zeta^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\geqslant 2\lambda_{\varepsilon}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

(b)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma
16
2
,

4\lvert\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{0}-H)\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rvert\leqslant 4\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\lVert H_{0}-H\rVert_{2}\leqslant 10\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(2m)}{md}}.

(c)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and part
1
,

4\lvert\langle\zeta_{t},(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rvert\leqslant 4\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\lVert H_{t}-H_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m})\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

Putting (a), (b) and (c) together and applying Assumption
3
2
that

\lambda_{\varepsilon}\geqslant 10\sqrt{\frac{\log(2m)}{md}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}),

we have

\lVert\nabla_{W}R_{t}\rVert^{2}_{\text{F}}\geqslant\left(2\lambda_{\varepsilon}-10\sqrt{\frac{\log(2m)}{md}}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m})\right)\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert^{2}_{2}\geqslant\lambda_{\varepsilon}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

3.

Differentiate both sides of
R_{t}=\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+R(f^{\star})
with respect to
t
and apply the chain rule to obtain

\frac{dR_{t}}{dt}=2\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\frac{d\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\quad\implies\quad\frac{d\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}=\frac{1}{2\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}\frac{dR_{t}}{dt}.

We apply the chain rule and part
2
to see that

\frac{dR_{t}}{dt}=\left\langle\nabla_{W}R_{t},\frac{dW}{dt}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}=-\lVert\nabla_{W}R_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}^{2}\leqslant-\lambda_{\varepsilon}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

Hence, substituting this into above,

\frac{d\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\leqslant-\frac{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}{2}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}.

4.

We apply Grönwall’s inequality and the fact that
\lVert\zeta_{0}\rVert_{2}=\lVert f^{\star}\rVert_{2}\leqslant 1
to see that

\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\zeta_{0}\rVert_{2}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\varepsilon}t\right)\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\varepsilon}t\right).

∎

Finally, we prove that
S_{\varepsilon}\subseteq[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
is inductive. Then we know from Appendix
B.5
that
S_{\varepsilon}=[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
.

Theorem 24
.

Suppose that Assumption
2
1
and Assumption
3
2
are satisfied. Then
S_{\varepsilon}\subseteq[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
is inductive.

Proof.

We prove each of (RI1), (RI2) and (RI3) for the set
S_{\varepsilon}
.

1.

Obvious.

2.

Fix some
T\in[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon})
, and suppose that
T\in S_{\varepsilon}
. Then we want to show that there exists some
\gamma>0
such that
[T,T+\gamma]\subseteq S_{\varepsilon}
. Since
T\in S_{\varepsilon}
, we have
\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}<\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}
for each
j=1,...,m
. Define

\gamma_{j}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}-\frac{\sqrt{md}\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}}{2\sqrt{2}}.

Then
\gamma_{j}>0
, and for all
t\in[T,T+\gamma_{j}]
,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\left\lVert\int^{t}_{T}\frac{d\mathbf{w}_{j}}{dt}dt\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\int^{t}_{T}\lVert\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j}}R_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\int^{t}_{T}\underbrace{\lVert\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j}}R_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:non_random}\ref{H_W}}}dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{md}}\underbrace{\int^{t}_{T}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}dt}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:approximation}\ref{zeta_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}(t-T)}{\sqrt{md}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}

\displaystyle<\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}.

Now take
\gamma=\min_{j\in\{1,...,m\}}\gamma_{j}
. Then
[T,T+\gamma]\subseteq S_{\varepsilon}
as required.

3.

Fix some
T\in(0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
and suppose that
[0,T)\subseteq S_{\varepsilon}
. Then we want to show that
T\in S_{\varepsilon}
. See that, for each
j\in\{1,...,m\}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}(0)\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{d\mathbf{w}_{j}}{dt}dt\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int^{T}_{0}\lVert\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j}}R_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\frac{2}{md}}\int^{T}_{0}\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}dt

by Lemma
15
2

\displaystyle<\sqrt{\frac{2}{md}}\int^{T}_{0}e^{-\frac{\lambda_{\varepsilon}t}{2}}dt\quad

by Lemma
23
4

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}.

Hence
T\in S_{\varepsilon}
as required.

Since all of (RI1), (RI2) and (RI3) are satisfied,
S_{\varepsilon}\subseteq[0,T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
is inductive.
∎

Now we show that
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
is large enough to ensure that
T_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\frac{2}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\log\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\leqslant T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
such that, for all
t\in[T_{\varepsilon},T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
, the approximation error is below the desired level:
\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}
.

See
8

Proof.

Recall from Section
D.2.4
that we had
\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}=\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+R(f^{\star})
, the population risk in this subspace. Differentiating both sides of this with respect to
t
using the chain rule gives us

\frac{d\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}}{dt}=2\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\frac{d\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}\qquad\implies\qquad\frac{d\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}{dt}=\frac{1}{2\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}\frac{d\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}}{dt}.

Here, see that, by the chain rule,

\frac{d\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}}{dt}=\left\langle\nabla_{W}\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t},\frac{d\tilde{W}^{L_{\varepsilon}}}{dt}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}=-\lVert\nabla_{W}\tilde{R}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}^{2}\leqslant 0.

Substituting this back into above, we know that
\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
is not increasing. Hence, by our choice of
L_{\varepsilon}
,

\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\varepsilon}

for all
t\geqslant 0
.

Now, as we perform gradient flow from
t=0
, we know that, by Lemma
23
4
,

\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\varepsilon}t\right)

up to
T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
. Then for all
t<T_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}>\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\geqslant 2\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{0}\rVert_{2}\geqslant 2\lVert\tilde{\zeta}^{L_{\varepsilon}}_{t}\rVert_{2},

which means
t<T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
and we can continue gradient flow with Lemma
23
4
continuing to hold. After we have reached
T_{\varepsilon}
, i.e., for all
t\in[T_{\varepsilon},T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}]
, we have

\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}

as required.
∎

D.6
Proof of Small Estimation Error

In this section, we assume that we are still on the high-probability event
E_{3}
of Appendix
D.3
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{3})\geqslant 1-\delta
, which means that we can assume all the results from Appendix
D.4
and
D.5
.

First, we prove the following decomposition of the estimation error.

Lemma 25
.

For any integer
U\geqslant 2
and for any
T>0
, we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{f}_{T}-f_{T}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=1}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-2}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2^{U}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U-1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\kern 5.0pt\left.-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U-1}(\zeta_{t_{U}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U}dt_{U-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
U
. We first look at the base case
U=2
. As noted before (e.g., in the proof of Lemma
17
1
), the vector
\sqrt{d}\mathbf{x}
is isotropic
(
Vershynin 2018
, p.45, Exercise 3.3.1)
. Then see that

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{f}_{T}-f_{T}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}[(\phi(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)\cdot\mathbf{x})-\phi(\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)\cdot\mathbf{x}))^{2}]}\qquad\text{triangle inequality}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}[((\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(T))\cdot\mathbf{x})^{2}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{dm}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}[((\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(T))\cdot(\sqrt{d}\mathbf{x}))^{2}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{dm}}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\lVert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(T)-\mathbf{w}_{j}(T)\rVert_{2}\qquad\text{\cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{vershynin2018high}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, p.43, Lemma 3.2.3)}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\hat{W}(T)-W(T)\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\hat{W}(T)-W(0)-(W(T)-W(0))\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{d\hat{W}}{dt}\Bigr|_{t_{1}}-\frac{dW}{dt}\Bigr|_{t_{1}}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t_{1}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}-\frac{1}{n}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{0}+\frac{1}{n}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{0}-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}-\langle G_{t_{1}},\zeta_{t_{1}}\rangle_{2}dt\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\int^{T}_{0}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}dt_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t_{1}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}-\frac{1}{n}\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{0}+\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}-\langle G_{t_{1}},\zeta_{t_{1}}\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t_{1}}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\langle G_{0}-G_{t_{1}},\zeta_{t_{1}}\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{t_{1}}-\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{t_{1}}-\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}.

(*)

Here, for the last term,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{t_{1}}-\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{d\hat{\bm{\xi}}}{dt_{2}}dt_{2}\right)-\left\langle G_{0},\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{d\zeta}{dt_{2}}dt_{2}\right\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert-\int^{T}_{0}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{2}{n}\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t_{2}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}dt_{2}+\left\langle G_{0},\int^{t_{1}}_{0}2H_{t_{2}}\zeta_{t_{2}}dt_{2}\right\rangle_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t_{2}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\langle G_{0},H_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}+\langle G_{0},H_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}-\langle G_{0},H_{t_{2}}\zeta_{t_{2}}\rangle_{2}dt_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\left[(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t_{2}}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}+\mathbf{H}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\left\langle G_{0},H_{0}(\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{t_{2}})+(H_{0}-H_{t_{2}})\zeta_{t_{2}}\right\rangle_{2}dt_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}n^{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0},(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},H_{0}(\zeta_{t_{2}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}.

Now, putting this into (*), we have

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{f}_{T}-f_{T}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{G}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}n^{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2T^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0},(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},H_{0}(\zeta_{t_{2}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{2}_{u=1}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{2}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-2}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{2}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{2-1}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{2}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{2-1}(\zeta_{t_{2}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{2}dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}.

So the base case
u=2
holds. Suppose that the claim is true for
u
, i.e.,the following holds:

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{f}_{T}-f_{T}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=1}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-2}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2^{U}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U-1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\kern 5.0pt\left.-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U-1}(\zeta_{t_{U}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U}dt_{U-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}.

(**)

Consider the last term involving the norm of an integral:

\displaystyle\frac{2^{U}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U-1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U-1}(\zeta_{t_{U}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U}dt_{U-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{U}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U-1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{d\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}}{dt_{U+1}}dt_{U+1}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\left\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U-1}\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{d\zeta}{dt_{U+1}}dt_{U+1}\right\rangle_{2}dt_{U}dt_{U-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U-1}}_{0}\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t_{U+1}}\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\left\langle G_{0},H^{U-1}_{0}H_{t_{U+1}}\zeta_{t_{U+1}}\right\rangle_{2}dt_{U+1}dt_{U}dt_{U-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}...\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t_{U+1}}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\left.\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})+\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U}(\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{t_{U+1}})\rangle_{2}+\langle G_{0},H^{U-1}_{0}(H_{0}-H_{t_{U+1}})\zeta_{t_{U+1}}\rangle_{2}dt_{U+1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T)^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}(U+1)!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H^{U}_{0}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{(2T)^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}(U+1)!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U-1}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(2T)^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}(U+1)!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U-1}(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}...\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U}(\zeta_{t_{U+1}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U+1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}.

Putting this into (**), we have

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{f}_{T}-f_{T}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U+1}_{u=1}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}+\frac{2T}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\langle G_{0}-G_{t},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U+1}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-2}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U+1}_{u=2}\frac{(2T)^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{0}-H_{t})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2^{U+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T}_{0}...\int^{t_{U}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U+1}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U+1}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\kern 5.0pt\left.-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U}(\zeta_{t_{U+1}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U+1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}.

So by induction, the result of the lemma is proven.
∎

We are finally ready to prove our estimation result.
See
9

Proof.

We will use the decomposition in Lemma
25
with
T=T_{\varepsilon}
and
U=U_{\varepsilon}
. We will consider each term appearing in the decomposition separately.

1.

See that

\displaystyle\frac{2^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T_{\varepsilon}}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U_{\varepsilon}-1}}_{0}\frac{1}{n^{U_{\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{U_{\varepsilon}-1}(\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\bm{\xi}_{0})dt_{U_{\varepsilon}}dt_{U_{\varepsilon}-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!n^{U_{\varepsilon}}}\underbrace{\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\rVert_{2}\lVert\mathbf{H}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{U_{\varepsilon}-1}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:probability_samples}\ref{spectralnorm}}}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\bm{\xi}_{0}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:overfitting}\ref{xi_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!n^{U_{\varepsilon}}}\frac{2^{2U_{\varepsilon}}n^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{d^{U_{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(8T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{d^{U_{\varepsilon}}U_{\varepsilon}!}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{14}\sqrt{\varepsilon}

by the definition of
U_{\varepsilon}
(see eqn. (
4.3
)).

2.

See that

\displaystyle\frac{2^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}}\left\lVert\int^{T_{\varepsilon}}_{0}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}...\int^{t_{U_{\varepsilon}-1}}_{0}\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U_{\varepsilon}-1}(\zeta_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}dt_{U_{\varepsilon}}dt_{U_{\varepsilon}-1}...dt_{1}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!}\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{U_{\varepsilon}-1}(\zeta_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!}\sqrt{\langle H_{0}^{U_{\varepsilon}}(\zeta_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\zeta_{0}),H_{0}^{U_{\varepsilon}-1}(\zeta_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\zeta_{0})\rangle_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!}\underbrace{\lVert H_{0}\rVert_{2}^{U_{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{2}}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:non_random}\ref{H_W}}}\underbrace{\lVert\zeta_{t_{U_{\varepsilon}}}-\zeta_{0}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:approximation}\ref{zeta_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{d}U_{\varepsilon}!}\frac{2}{(2d)^{U_{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}T_{\varepsilon}^{U_{\varepsilon}}}{d^{U_{\varepsilon}}U_{\varepsilon}!}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{14}\sqrt{\varepsilon},

also by the definition of
U_{\varepsilon}
.

3.

See that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\lVert\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\sqrt{\langle H_{0}^{u-2}(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t},H_{0}^{u-1}(H_{t}-H_{0})\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\underbrace{\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:approximation}\ref{zeta_t}}}\underbrace{\lVert H_{0}\rVert_{2}^{u-\frac{3}{2}}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:non_random}\ref{H_W}}}\underbrace{\lVert H_{t}-H_{0}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:approximation}\ref{H_tH_0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\frac{1}{(2d)^{u-\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{md^{3}}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m})

\displaystyle=\frac{6+\sqrt{2\log m}}{\sqrt{md}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{T_{\varepsilon}^{u}}{u!d^{u}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{14},

by Assumption
3
4
.

4.

See that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-2}(\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\underbrace{\lVert\mathbf{G}_{0}\rVert_{2}\lVert\mathbf{H}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{u-2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:probability_samples}\ref{spectralnorm}}}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\mathbf{H}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{0}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:overfitting}\ref{hatH_tH_0}}}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:overfitting}\ref{xi_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{n^{u}u!}\frac{2^{2u-3}n^{u-\frac{3}{2}}}{d^{u-\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{4n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{\sqrt{md}}\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{d}(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{2\sqrt{m}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{(8T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!d^{u}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{6+\sqrt{2\log m}}{\sqrt{md}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{T_{\varepsilon}^{u}}{u!d^{u}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{14},

by Assumption
3
4
.

5.

Note that

\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\text{diag}[\mathbf{a}]\left(\phi^{\prime}\left(\hat{W}(t)X^{\top}\right)-\phi^{\prime}\left(\hat{W}(0)X^{\top}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n},

and so for each
i=1,...,n
, the squared Euclidean norm of the
i^{\text{th}}
column of
\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}
is

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\text{diag}[\mathbf{a}]\left(\phi^{\prime}(\hat{W}(t)\mathbf{x}_{i})-\phi^{\prime}(\hat{W}(0)\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}a_{j}^{2}\left(\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})-\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\left\{\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\right\}.

Now we apply (
B.1
), (
B.1
) and Lemma
17
1
to see that

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert((\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0})*X^{\top})^{\top}((\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0})*X^{\top})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert(XX^{\top})\odot((\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0})^{\top}(\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{J}}_{0}))\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert X\rVert^{2}_{2}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\{\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4n}{d}\max_{i\in\{1,...,n\}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\{\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\}.

Here, for each
i=1,...,n
and
j=1,...,m
, in order for
\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i})
, there must be some
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
on the weight trajectory, such that
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i}=0
and

\lVert\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}\leqslant 32\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}.

But by Lemma
16
3
, there only exist at most
\sqrt{md}(34+\sqrt{\log m})
neurons such that this happens. Hence,

\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leqslant\frac{4n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}{\sqrt{md}}.

Taking the square root, we have

\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{0}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}}{(md)^{1/4}}.

Now see that

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{0})\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{n\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\mathbf{G}}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{0}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{above}}\underbrace{\lVert\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:overfitting}\ref{xi_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{m\sqrt{d}}\frac{2\sqrt{n(34+\sqrt{\log m})}}{(md)^{1/4}}\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{4T_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{34+\sqrt{\log m}}}{(md^{3})^{1/4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{6+\sqrt{2\log m}}{\sqrt{md}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{T_{\varepsilon}^{u}}{u!d^{u}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{14},

by Assumption
3
4
.

6.

Define an integral operator
\tilde{H}_{t}:L^{2}(\rho_{d-1})\rightarrow L^{2}(\rho_{d-1})
by

\tilde{H}_{t}(f)(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}[\langle(G_{t}-G_{0})(\mathbf{x}),(G_{t}-G_{0})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\rangle_{\text{F}}f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})].

An explicit expression for
\tilde{H}_{t}(f)(\mathbf{x})
is

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left(\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})\right)\left(\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right)f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right],

and so by applying Lemma
12
, and recalling the linear operator
\Xi:L^{2}(\rho_{d-1})\rightarrow L^{2}(\rho_{d-1})
defined by
\Xi(f)(x)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{x}^{\prime}f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})]
with
\lVert\Xi\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2d}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{H}_{t}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2d}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left(\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})-\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2d}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{1}{m}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\mathbf{1}\left\{\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})\right\}.

Here, for each
j=1,...,m
, in order for
\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(t)\cdot\mathbf{x})\neq\phi^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\cdot\mathbf{x})
, there must be some
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
on the weight trajectory, such that
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}=0
and

\lVert\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}_{j}(0)\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{md}}.

But by Lemma
16
4
, there only exist at most
\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{d}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m})
neurons such that this happens. Hence,

\lVert\tilde{H}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}d^{3/2}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}(3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}).

Then see that

\displaystyle\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\lVert\langle G_{t}-G_{0},\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}[(G_{t}-G_{0})(\mathbf{x})\zeta_{t}(\mathbf{x})]\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\sqrt{\langle\zeta_{t},\tilde{H}_{t}\zeta_{t}\rangle_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\underbrace{\sqrt{\lVert\tilde{H}_{t}\rVert_{2}}}_{\text{above}}\underbrace{\lVert\zeta_{t}\rVert_{2}}_{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:approximation}\ref{zeta_t}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2T_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{d}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}(md^{3})^{1/4}\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}}\sqrt{3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}T_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{3\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{\log m}}}{(md^{5})^{1/4}\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{6+\sqrt{2\log m}}{\sqrt{md}\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=2}\frac{T_{\varepsilon}^{u}}{u!d^{u}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{14},

by Assumption
3
4
.

7.

We have from Lemma
18
2
that
\lVert V_{u}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\leqslant 8\sqrt{\frac{\log(nu)}{\lfloor\frac{n}{u}\rfloor}}
for all
u=1,...,U_{\varepsilon}
. Then see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=1}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n^{u}}\mathbf{G}_{0}\mathbf{H}_{0}^{u-1}\bm{\xi}_{0}-\langle G_{0},H_{0}^{u-1}\zeta_{0}\rangle_{2}\right\rVert_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=1}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\lVert V_{u}\rVert_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{8}{\sqrt{d}}\sum^{U_{\varepsilon}}_{u=1}\frac{(2T_{\varepsilon})^{u}}{u!}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nu)}{\lfloor\frac{n}{u}\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{14}

as required, where the last inequality follows by Assumption
3
3
.

Putting it all together,
\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}
is bounded by a sum of seven terms each bounded by
\frac{1}{14}\sqrt{\varepsilon}
, so

\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{2}

as required.
∎

D.7
Putting it all Together: Generalization and Benign Overfitting

Bringing together Theorem
8
and Theorem
9
, we have a generalization result.
See
10

Proof.

We have the approximation-estimation decomposition from eqn. (
4.4
):

\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f^{\star}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\zeta_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}.

Here, Theorem
8
gives us
\lVert\zeta_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, and Theorem
9
gives us
\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
. Thence we have

\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f^{\star}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\zeta_{T_{\varepsilon}}\rVert_{2}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon.

Since,
R(\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}})-R(f^{\star})=\lVert\hat{f}_{T_{\varepsilon}}-f^{\star}\rVert_{2}^{2}
, we get the claimed result.
∎

Finally, bringing together Theorem
7
and Theorem
10
, we have the benign overfitting result.
See
11

Proof.

This is an immediate corollary of Theorem
7
and Theorem
10
.
∎

D.8
Additional Experimental Evaluations

In this section, we provide additional experimental evaluations.

Synthetic Data Experiments.

For the synthetic data experiments, we use
d=3
, and the first eigenfunction of the NTK operator
H
as
f^{\star}
, i.e., the spherical harmonic of order
1
, obtained by the Rodrigues representation
(
Müller 1998
, p.22, Lemma 4)
on the Legendre polynomials
(
Müller 1998
, p.16, (
\mathsection
2.32) & Lemma 2)
(see also Section
D.2.3
). For
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have:
f^{\star}(\mathbf{x})=P_{1}(3;x_{3})=x_{3}
,
where we denoted by
P_{1}(3;\cdot)
the Legendre polynomial of order 1 in dimension 3. In other words, given a point on the sphere,
f^{\star}
simply maps it to the value of the third coordinate. By construction, this gives
L_{\varepsilon}=1
and
\lambda_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{12}
(c.f. eqn. (
4.1
)). We use
m=750000
. The
\mathbf{x}_{i}
’s are sampled uniformly from unit sphere. The
y_{i}
’s (the target variables during the training process) are constructed as
f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
plus mean-zero Gaussian noise with standard deviation
0.2
.

Figure 4
:
Synthetic Data Experiment: Risk vs. model complexity plot on synthetic data. Increasing both the sample size

n
and the number of training iterations simultaneously allows for reduction of both empirical and excess risks.

Figure 5
:
Synthetic Data Experiment: The average iteration at which the excess risk crosses and stays over the empirical evaluated over
10
runs with different random initializations to the neural network. The bars indicate the standard deviation on the iteration number. Note the clear shift to the right and down.

In Figure
5
, we plot empirical (dashed) and excess (solid) risk curves against gradient descent iterations

T
for various sample sizes
n
, using matching colors for each
n
. The results are similar to what we observed in Section
4.3
. The empirical risk decreases with
T
, with smaller
n
yielding stronger overfitting. Excess risk exhibits a U-shaped curve, first decreasing then increasing. The
\star
markers denote the point where excess risk overtakes empirical risk and remains higher. These
\star
markers shift lower and rightward as
n
increases. This supports our theory that, with sufficient data and appropriate model complexity, both risks can be simultaneously minimized.
16
16

16

We could equally analyze the trough of the excess risk curve and reach the same conclusion; we focus on the crossover points for convenience, since both risks are equal at those points.
We also perform multiple runs, with different initializations. These results are presented in Figure
5
.

Experiments on Abalone Dataset.

We now discuss additional experiments on the Abalone dataset disuccsed in Section
4.3
. In Figure
6
, we plot the risk vs. model complexity curves (with
m=10000
) by varying the noise levels. We add mean-zero Gaussian noise with standard deviation in
\{0.1,0.2,0.3\}
to the target variable in the training data. The results are consistent with our previous findings. As expected, for same
n
, across the various plots in Figure
6
, we see that higher noise levels shift the crossing point (marked by
\star
) to later iterations.

In Figure
7
, we show the result across multiple runs, with different random initializations to the neural network.

(a) Gaussian noise (mean-zero, std. dev
0.1
)

(b) Gaussian noise (mean-zero, std. dev
0.2
)

(c) Gaussian noise (mean-zero, std. dev
0.3
)

Figure 6
:
Abalone Data Experiment: Results with varying noise levels. The figure (b) is duplicated from Section
4.3
.

Figure 7
:
Abalone Data Experiment: The average iteration at which the excess risk crosses and stays over the empirical evaluated over
10
runs with different random initializations. This is for the setting discussed in Section
4.3
, with Gaussian noise (mean-zero, std. dev
0.2
). Again notice the shift to
the right and down of where the crossing occurs.

Figure 8
:
Wine Data Experiment: Risk vs. model complexity plot with varying sample size
n
. We use
m=100000
.

Experiments on Wine Dataset.

For our next real data experiment, we use the Wine dataset
(
Aeberhard and Forina 1992
)
where the input dimension
d=11
. The goal is to predict wine quality from various features. We standardized inputs and targets, and add Gaussian noise during training. Figure
8
shows the risk vs. model complexity plot, leading to the same conclusions as with our previous experiments.
